# Foreword 

For things to function effectively, a solid system and structure are essential. Without them, dysfunction and chaos can easily arise. This is why establishing a strong foundation in doctrine is crucial for fully living as a healthy Christian.

That's why "*Principles of Faith*" will serve as a powerful resource for believers. From the very first chapter, which addresses the importance of sound doctrine, to the rest of the over 300 pages filled with teachings and activities about the church and our roles within it, as well as understanding and recognizing God's voice, this book is invaluable. It will be an exceptional tool in the classroom for teaching and preparing leaders with a solid foundation in doctrine, while also being highly beneficial for individual readers.

Andrew has been an integral part of my inner circle for many years, and I continue to be impressed by his talents and his approach to understanding kingdom matters. He possesses a genuine passion for ensuring that the church operates as a healthy representation of Christ on Earth. I take great pride in the work he has accomplished with this book, and having collaborated with Him for a significant time, I can confidently attest that he has invested considerable time and thought into this process, just as he does with everything in his life.

***Evangelist Robert Newton***

***Founder and President of Powerhouse Ministries***

# Preface

## Building a Foundation that Lasts

I have always had a fascination with questions. Why is *something* true? What made it that way? How do we know? How are we sure? If *something* is true, what else is true? What is false? This fascination goes beyond a desire to be correct on a surface level, but to know deeply and precisely, and to be able to clearly articulate the new gained understanding.

While communication is common, effective and precise communication is challenging. To effectively communicate a message the author and originator must have an idea. However, the idea alone like water on a hard surface-its edges and boundaries are poorly defined, fluid, and easily moved around.

We use words, terms, and phrases to transform the raw idea from vaguely shaped to precisely shaped. Words give our idea a boundary, they help us distinguish and separate between what the idea is, and what it is not. Now our shaped idea must be transmitted and received through some medium. Whether in long form text, like this book, or short form text, like a social media post, or in oral form, like a conversation or a video recording, the medium transmits the now well-formed idea to the recipient.

So far, we’ve only discussed the originator and author of the idea. For an idea to be useful, the recipient must receive, decode, and understand the message accurately. The transmitted and received messages should align closely.

Every great human work is the by-product of effective communication. One man’s labor can only accomplish so much. To really build or perform something great, many men (speaking here generically of mankind) must labor together in a shared purpose, with a shared idea. Barring naturally occurring disasters (like a volcanic eruption) we also know that the greatest calamities to ever occur usually did so in significant part because of missing, broken or distorted communication. Truly life and death are in the power of the tongue (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Proverbs" data-chapter="18" data-verses="21" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Proverbs 18:21</span>).

What we believe and how we communicate it has the power to build a person, a family, a community, a nation, or has the power to equally destroy them. What we believe is the foundation for our actions and is the blueprint for what we will do, who we will be, how we will exist. Because belief is expressed through action and communication, it defines the role we play in existence. Our beliefs shape not just our lives, but to a degree the lives of everyone around us.

We must establish our beliefs accurately and learn to share them effectively. Jesus said as much when he said to His disciples:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="18-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Matthew 28:18-20</u>** (NKJV) ***18** … "All authority has been given to Me in heaven and on earth.* ***19** Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, **20** teaching them to observe all things that I have commanded you; and lo, I am with you always, even to the end of the age." Amen.*

</span>


Many Christians today desire a stronger faith but lack a clear foundation to build upon. Without solid doctrine, we lack the firm foundation we need to stand strong; we risk being swayed by false teaching, discouraged in trials, or unprepared for the work God has called us to do. This book is designed to equip you with the foundational teachings of Scripture-truths that strengthen your faith, deepen your walk with Christ, and empower you to live a Spirit-filled life.

The Apostle Paul urged believers to be “*rooted and built up in Him and established in the faith*” (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Colossians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Colossians 2:7</span>). To grow into mature, Spirit-led believers, we must first have deep roots in God’s truth. This is why doctrine matters. It is more than information; it is the framework that shapes how we live and respond to God.

## Why This Book, Who Is it For?

The teachings in this book are meant to educate and encourage you, the reader, in the foundational doctrines of the New Testament church. It is my hope that these beliefs will convince and inspire your walk of faith to plant deep roots in Christ and to come to know Him personally.

These teachings are framed and shaped by my experience in the church, particularly in Charismatic or Pentecostal manifestations. The composition of the teachings in this book builds upon my own personal study of the scripture, formal and informal education, and from my experience in close to twenty years involvement in ministry, much of that time being an ordained minister. My background includes youth and children’s ministry, evangelistic outreach and revival ministry, discipleship, pastoral counseling, and church and ministry administration.

This book serves believers seeking to strengthen their faith or disciple others in their spiritual growth. Specifically:

- **New Believers** - Those who need a clear foundation to start their Christian journey.

- **Growing Christians** - Those seeking to deepen their understanding of God and Scripture while walking in greater obedience and faith.

- **Teachers, Mentors, and Small Group Leaders** - Those preparing to disciple others, whether one-on-one, in small groups, or from the pulpit.

Each chapter is designed to equip believers both **personally** and **practically**, making it ideal for personal growth as well as group teaching. Whether you're using this book to strengthen your own walk with God or to train others, the content is structured for easy application and Spirit-led growth.

## How to Use This Book

The chapters follow a logical progression, beginning with foundational truths like **God**, **sin**, **repentance**, and **faith**, and moving into the deeper work of the **Holy Spirit**, **discipleship**, and **spiritual authority**. Each topic stands alone but also builds upon the previous chapters, making it easy to work through step-by-step or focus on specific areas as needed.

For **individuals**, this book serves as a guide to understanding and applying Scripture. For **teachers**, it provides material to prepare lessons, lead discussions, and disciple others effectively. Personal reflection questions, group discussion questions for facilitation, group activities, and word studies are included to help deepen understanding and encourage practical steps of obedience.

## How will we study?

We have been given a primary source for sound doctrine, the teachings of the Holy Spirit, inspired and recorded in scripture, passed down through the teachings and tradition of the church. This book will take essential teachings, present them in easy-to-understand format and conclude each chapter with a personal reflection, group facilitation questions, and optional group activities.

Let’s explore each element in a bit more depth:

### The Holy Spirit


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="John" data-chapter="16" data-verses="13-14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>John 16:13-14</u>** (NKJV) ***13** However, when He, the Spirit of truth, has come, He will guide you into all truth; for He will not speak on His own authority, but whatever He hears He will speak; and He will tell you things to come.* ***14** He will glorify Me, for He will take of what is Mine and declare it to you.*

</span>


God, through the Holy Spirit, inspired all recorded Scripture, but God’s work is not done! When we read and study Scripture the same Holy Spirit that authored it, illuminates our study, and makes our understanding! The Holy Spirit is our comforter and helper, and His role is unchanged today. He helps us understand the scriptures and walk in truth with Him.

### The Scriptures


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="2 Timothy" data-chapter="3" data-verses="16" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>2 Timothy 3:16</u>** (NKJV) *All Scripture is given by inspiration of God, and is profitable for doctrine, for reproof, for correction, for instruction in righteousness,*

</span>


The collection of scriptures we have today reflects the written words of God. God, by His Spirit, inspired select men and revealed to them His words. In the earliest period, before Moses, not much was formally written down; instead, oral tradition preserved much of God's interactions with humanity over centuries. The scriptures state that God used Moses to write the Torah (the first five books of the Old Testament), marking the beginning of a more formal written record of God's instructions and covenant with His people.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="24" data-verses="4" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Exodus 24:4</u>** (NKJV) *And Moses wrote all the words of the LORD. And he rose early in the morning, and built an altar at the foot of the mountain, and twelve pillars according to the twelve tribes of Israel.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Numbers" data-chapter="33" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Numbers 33:2</u>** (NKJV) *Now Moses wrote down the starting points of their journeys at the command of the LORD. And these are their journeys according to their starting points*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Deuteronomy" data-chapter="31" data-verses="9,11" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Deuteronomy 31:9,11</u>** (NKJV) **9** *So Moses wrote this law and delivered it to the priests, the sons of Levi, who bore the ark of the covenant of the LORD, and to all the elders of Israel…* ***11** when all Israel comes to appear before the LORD your God in the place which He chooses, you shall read this law before all Israel in their hearing.*

</span>


As history continued, God maintained His relationship with His people, and various prophets, leaders, and scribes recorded these interactions and teachings. This process resulted in what we now recognize as the Old Testament.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="30" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Isaiah 30:8</u>** (NKJV) * Now go, write it before them on a tablet, And note it on a scroll, That it may be for time to come, Forever and ever*

</span>


<x-scripture ref="<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="8" data-verses="31-35" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 8:31-35</span>" type="quote" version="NKJV" id="p34-1"/>


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Jeremiah" data-chapter="36" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Jeremiah 36:2</u>** (NKJV) *Take a scroll of a book and write on it all the words that I have spoken to you against Israel, against Judah, and against all the nations, from the day I spoke to you, from the days of Josiah even to this day.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Habakkuk" data-chapter="2" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Habakkuk 2:2</u>** (NKJV) *Then the LORD answered me and said: "Write the vision And make it plain on tablets, That he may run who reads it.*

</span>


Following the ministry of Jesus, numerous eyewitnesses and apostles documented accounts of His life, ministry, death, and resurrection. They also wrote letters, or epistles, that addressed doctrinal and practical matters within the early Christian communities. These writings, divinely inspired, were carefully recorded, compiled, and preserved through the centuries, forming what is now known as the Bible.

Generally, we will use the NKJV translation but also may use a different translation where it makes the passage clearer. *Scripture references will be italicized to aid in reading.*

I strongly suggest using multiple translations whenever possible, to ensure the concept is clearly understood in your mind. I personally find translations like the Amplified bible to be of benefit, not for its pure textual accuracy, but to help me understand in plain English what the scriptures are communicating.

We’ll take time to explore certain key Bible words (**word studies**) so we can better understand what God is saying and how it applies to our lives. I recommend learning to use electronic bible resources like: E-Sword (<https://www.e-sword.net/>), or the Blue Letter Bible ([<u>https://www.blueletterbible.org/</u>](https://www.blueletterbible.org/)). Such resources will also give you access to a Strong's Concordance.

Strong’s Concordance helps us understand what the Bible meant in its original language, so we can hear more clearly what God is saying. The Old Testament (sometimes abbreviated OT in this book) was authored in Hebrew. The New Testament (sometimes abbreviated NT in this book) was authored in Greek. The Strong’s number will be indicated: **Hxxx** (Hebrew) or **Gyyy** (Greek).

### The Teachings of the Church


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Matthew 28:20</u>** (NKJV) *teaching them to observe all things that I have commanded you; and lo, I am with you always, even to the end of the age." Amen.*

</span>


The teachings I present in this book are not new or novel. We should be skeptical of ‘new’ never-before-seen-or-heard teachings regarding our Christian faith. Rather this book is a compilation and explanation of teachings that have been present since the beginning.

This book is not a study of church history, nor is it a comparison on Christian denominations. Pick up any two books on theology written by different authors with divergent denominational backgrounds and while you will see great harmony in much of their work, you will see notable division. I will not claim inerrancy, but I will claim that this work is well studied, peer reviewed by faithful men and women (see Acknowledgements section), and well supported by the scriptures and by well accepted Pentecostal tradition.

I implore you to read the scriptures for yourself and commit to their mastery. God’s Word promises that when we seek Him, we will find Him (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Jeremiah" data-chapter="29" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Jeremiah 29:13</span>). I invite you to approach this book with faith and expectation. As you walk through these teachings, I pray you’ll not only grow in understanding but also experience the power of the Holy Spirit working in and through you. My hope is that you will be restored, strengthened, and empowered to fulfill God’s purpose in your life.

# Chapter 1 - Sound Doctrine Matters

Doctrine simply means the core truths we believe and teach-truths passed on by faithful people of God, rooted in Scripture, and lived out in our daily walk.

When Jesus instructed His church to ‘teach’, He was instructing them to share His doctrine.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="18-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Matthew 28:18-20</u>** (NKJV) ***18** And Jesus came and spoke to them, saying, "All authority has been given to Me in heaven and on earth.* ***19** Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit,* ***20** teaching them to observe all things that I have commanded you; and lo, I am with you always, even to the end of the age." Amen.*

</span>


In other words, Jesus is instructing all of us to learn His teachings and to replicate them in others.

Jesus is the Way, the Truth, and the Life (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="14" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 14:6</span>), fully embodying God’s truth, wisdom, and revelation. This embodiment, the Word (logos), existed from the beginning and became flesh to dwell among us (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="1" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 1:1</span>, 14). The structured teachings of Jesus is His doctrine (***didaskalia***), in observing (obeying, guarding, protecting) His commands (***entolē)*** we become His disciples (***mathētēs***).

Imagine a master architect, the greatest builder in all history, whose vision, knowledge, and wisdom surpass all others. He alone understands the perfect design for an enduring, unshakable structure. His understanding is not derived from others-it is inherent within Him. He is the source of all architectural wisdom, and every sound building finds its origin in His design. This architect represents Jesus, the Logos-the full and complete embodiment of God’s truth, wisdom, and revelation (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="1" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 1:1</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="14" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 14:6</span>).

To bring His design to life, the architect creates a **detailed blueprint**-a structured plan that contains the principles, proportions, and foundational truths necessary for constructing a building that will stand firm. Without this blueprint, no one could build correctly. This blueprint represents Jesus’ structured teaching, His doctrine **(Didaskalia)-**the framework through which divine truth is understood and applied (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Titus" data-chapter="2" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Titus 2:1</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Timothy" data-chapter="4" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Timothy 4:13</span>). His doctrine is not merely theoretical knowledge; it is the means by which His wisdom is communicated and preserved so that all who follow Him can build their lives on the right foundation.

However, a blueprint alone is not enough. The architect also provides **precise instructions**, detailing how each part of the structure should be assembled, where each beam must be placed, and how every element should align with His perfect design. These specific instructions represent His commands **(Entolē**)-the actionable directives that govern obedience (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="14" data-verses="15" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 14:15</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 28:20</span>). His commands are not arbitrary rules; they are the necessary steps to ensure the building stands strong, aligned with the original blueprint. To ignore or alter His commands would be to compromise the integrity of the entire structure.

Now, imagine that the architect gathers a group of **apprentices**-those who desire to learn from Him, build under His guidance, and one day teach others how to construct buildings that endure. These apprentices study His blueprint carefully, listen to His instructions attentively, and apply His commands faithfully in their work. They do not build according to their own ideas but according to the master’s design. These apprentices represent the disciples **(Mathētēs**)-those who observe, obey, and internalize Jesus’ teachings, aligning their lives with His Word (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="19-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 28:19-20</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="8" data-verses="31" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 8:31</span>). The more they study under the architect, the more skilled they become, eventually teaching and training others in His ways.

## Why Should We Study Doctrine?

Jesus’ teaching is the bedrock of our faith! He is the master architect who has a perfect design and blueprint. Only by studying the blueprints, obeying the architect’s specific instructions, and developing a strong working relationship can we build a life that will stand in the storms of life.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="127" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Psalm 127:1</u>** (NKJV) …*Unless the LORD builds the house, They labor in vain who build it; Unless the LORD guards the city, The watchman stays awake in vain.*

</span>


We cannot expect our lives to stand strong in the face of difficulties, challenges, tribulations, persecutions, or just ordinary life if we have not built our lives on the strong rock of faith.

It takes more than a gym membership to make a person healthy and physically fit. You must continue in the discipline of the gym to get the full benefits of membership. Likewise, we cannot merely attend a church or casually claim to be Christians, we all must become educated, trained, equipped, disciplined in the faith, only then will we realize the full benefits of membership!

Jesus’ charge extends beyond us. It’s great that we become whole, healthy, disciplined, submitted to God, but we must teach, train, equip, lead, and disciple others into the faith as well!

Why should we study doctrine and become disciplined? To build our lives on the rock!

However, this is more than just a benefit, it also stands as a warning to us. If we do not build our lives on the rock, we build our lives on the sand, and a strong storm will break our house. (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="7" data-verses="24-27" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 7:24-27</span>).

Our beliefs dictate our actions and our destiny. Proverbs teaches us that as a man thinks, so is he! (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Proverbs" data-chapter="23" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Proverbs 23:7</span>). We don’t get to stay neutral-we’re all building on something. Jesus calls us to build on the rock so we can stand strong and walk in His salvation. The promise of truth and freedom is for us!


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="John" data-chapter="8" data-verses="31-32" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>John 8:31-32</u>** (NKJV) ***31** Then Jesus said to those Jews who believed Him, "If you abide in My word, you are My disciples indeed.* ***32** And you shall know the truth, and the truth shall make you free."*

</span>


Being Jesus' disciple is only possible by continuing in His word (his logos)! The result of continuing in His word is our knowledge of the truth, which makes us free!

Disciples, having been taught discipline, can carry out the master’s commandments. Every person in the New Testament church is called to:

1.  Be a disciple -\> subject ourselves to the discipline of sound doctrine.

2.  Make disciples -\> teach others to be subject to the discipline of sound doctrine.

### How Do We Go About Building Our Lives On the Rock?

Don’t worry, we do not have to go this alone.

1.  God has given us His Holy Spirit who will lead us and guide us into all truth (John 14).

2.  God has given us His recorded words in the scriptures.

3.  God has given us spiritually mature gifted leaders to help build us up, make us mature, keep us safe, and organize us into doing good work.

Christianity is a team sport, and we have a perfect coach who knows exactly what we need. He has given us specialists who know through experience how to help us grow and mature, and in time you too can be one of them!


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Ephesians" data-chapter="4" data-verses="11-16" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Ephesians 4:11-16</u>** (NKJV)
>
>***11** And He Himself gave some to be apostles, some prophets, some evangelists, and some pastors and teachers,*
>
>***12** for the equipping of the saints for the work of ministry, for the edifying of the body of Christ,*
>
>***13**  till we all come to the unity of the faith and of the knowledge of the Son of God, to a perfect man, to the measure of the stature of the fullness of Christ;*
>
>***14** that we should no longer be children, tossed to and fro and carried about with every wind of doctrine, by the trickery of men, in the cunning craftiness of deceitful plotting,*
>
>***15** but, speaking the truth in love, may grow up in all things into Him who is the head-Christ-*
>
>***16** from whom the whole body, joined and knit together by what every joint supplies, according to the effective working by which every part does its share, causes growth of the body for the edifying of itself in love.*

</span>


**An important note:** the word ‘some’ indicates that not all of us are called with a gift of apostle, prophet, evangelist, pastor, or teacher but we are all given ministry gifts, talents, and special roles (discussed later in the chapter on the church).

God has given the church these special gifts to bring us to maturity, so we can and are:

- Able to do work and bear fruit

- Unified in the faith

- Full of the knowledge of God

- Mature

- Not like children

Without sound doctrine we will be <u>children, tossed about and carried off.</u>

Children are an important part of the family of God, but children are inherently:

- Immature - unable to bear fruit or reproduce.

- Naive - unable to discern between good and bad actors.

- Self-centered - concerned with the temporal and the now.

- Defenseless - vulnerable to harm.

### Sound doctrine Provides a Defense Against the Wickedness of the World

We do not have the luxury of being spiritually neutral in this life. We are either influenced by the Kingdom of God, or the Kingdom of Darkness.

Paul charged his spiritual son in the Lord Timothy saying:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="2 Timothy" data-chapter="4" data-verses="1-4" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>2 Timothy 4:1-4</u>** (KVJ) I *charge thee therefore before God, and the Lord Jesus Christ, who shall judge the quick and the dead at his appearing and his kingdom; Preach the word; be instant in season, out of season; reprove, rebuke, exhort with all longsuffering and doctrine. <u>For the time will come when they will not endure sound doctrine</u>; but after their own lusts shall they heap to themselves teachers, having itching ears; And they shall turn away their ears from the truth, and shall be turned unto fables. (emphasis added)*

</span>


Timothy, an aspiring leader in the church, was instructed to have a great depth of doctrinal understanding. Paul warns Timothy that there is a time coming when men will not endure sound doctrine and will fall away! Paul in the previous chapter of his letter to Timothy clarifies the nature and character of these men who will not endure sound doctrine:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="2 Timothy" data-chapter="3" data-verses="1-9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>2 Timothy 3:1-9</u>** (NKJV)
>
>***1** But know this, that in the last days perilous times will come:*
>
>***2** For men will be lovers of themselves, lovers of money, boasters, proud, blasphemers, disobedient to parents, unthankful, unholy,*
>
>***3** unloving, unforgiving, slanderers, without self-control, brutal, despisers of good,*
>
>***4** traitors, headstrong, haughty, lovers of pleasure rather than lovers of God,*
>
>***5** having a form of godliness but denying its power. And from such people turn away!*
>
>***6** For of this sort are those who creep into households and make captives of gullible women loaded down with sins, led away by various lusts,*
>
>***7** always learning and never able to come to the knowledge of the truth.*
>
>***8** Now as Jannes and Jambres resisted Moses, so do these also resist the truth: men of corrupt minds, disapproved concerning the faith;*
>
>***9** but they will progress no further, for their folly will be manifest to all, as theirs also was*.

</span>


The crux of this message is that perilous times will come. Men will be so self-absorbed that despite all their ‘teaching’ they will not submit to sound doctrine, will deny the power of God, and will walk with a corrupt and reprobate mind. Paul urges Timothy to be well prepared to preach sound doctrine.

As disciples we study and subject ourselves to the discipline of sound doctrine, this provides a defense against the wickedness of the world, we become full grown mature men and women of God, able to defend ourselves against the snares of the enemy and able to protect those around us that have not yet come to full maturity.

We must be careful and diligent in our study, sound teaching of the scriptures can be led astray by several perverse influences.

Sound doctrine can become twisted by the traditions of men. We see this truth evident from the need of the great reformers of Protestantism and in the history of the Old Testament. God raised men up to restore the fundamental truth that had become perverted by the traditions of men.

The scriptures warn us:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Colossians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Colossians 2:8</u>** (NKJV) *Beware lest anyone cheat you through philosophy and empty deceit, according to the tradition of men, according to the basic principles of the world, and not according to Christ.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="7" data-verses="9,13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Mark 7:9,13</u>** (NKJV) **9** *He said to them, "All too well you reject the commandment of God, that you may keep your tradition…* ***13** making the word of God of no effect through your tradition which you have handed down. And many such things you do."*

</span>


Men can twist sound doctrine through private interpretation. This is sadly clearly visible in the countless death cults and false messiahs that have come in each generation. These evil and corrupt leaders all claim to be God’s special representative with God’s special knowledge and refuse to submit themselves to the authority of Jesus and the boundaries set in the scriptures. The Spirit warns us:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="2 Peter" data-chapter="1" data-verses="20-21" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>2 Peter 1:20-21</u>** (NKJV) **20** *knowing this first, that no prophecy of Scripture is of any private interpretation,* ***21** for prophecy never came by the will of man, but holy men of God spoke as they were moved by the Holy Spirit.*

</span>


All truth and revelation are from the Spirit of Truth. God’s Spirit is not the author of confusion, so no teaching or revelation is to violate the scriptures that have been given us, inspired, and authored by the Holy Spirit.

In both cases, whether through group tradition or private interpretation we know that the spirit at work to deceive the church is the devil:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="1 Timothy" data-chapter="4" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>1 Timothy 4:1</u>** (NKJV) *Now the Spirit expressly says that in latter times some will depart from the faith, giving heed to deceiving spirits and doctrines of demons,*

</span>


Just like a parent must protect their children, mature disciples protect the immature in the faith from the destruction in this world. The end result of this maturity is our ability to bear much fruit!


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="John" data-chapter="15" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>John 15:8</u>** (NKJV) * By this My Father is glorified, that you bear much fruit; so you will be My disciples.*

</span>


What are His doctrines, what will we study?

In this work we will primarily study foundational doctrines of the New Testament church. These doctrines are foundational in that scriptures refer to them as such, but more so because they provide a strong foundation for the learner to build their life upon.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Hebrews" data-chapter="6" data-verses="1-2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Hebrews 6:1-2</u>** (NKJV) ***1** Therefore, leaving the discussion of the elementary principles of Christ, let us go on to perfection, not laying again the foundation of repentance from dead works and of faith toward God, **2** of the doctrine of baptisms, of laying on of hands, of resurrection of the dead, and of eternal judgment.*

</span>


 The core doctrines we will study are:

1.  Principle doctrines of the Christ (the Anointed One)

2.  Repentance from dead works

3.  Faith towards God

4.  Doctrine of baptisms

5.  Laying on of hands

6.  Resurrection of the dead

7.  Eternal judgment

We will also study core elements of our Christian walk:

- Sanctification by the Holy Spirit

- The Church and our role in it

- Making disciples

- Spiritual authority

We will study each of these in the context of the church, and study them so we can ‘go onto perfection (maturity)’. Our study will focus on the various perspectives and aspects of the Godhead as revealed to us: God the Father, God the Son, and God the Holy Spirit.

## Personal Reflection Questions 

1.  Why does sound doctrine matter in your personal walk with God?

2.  What does it mean to be “rooted and built up in Him” (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Colossians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Colossians 2:7</span>)? How does doctrine help with this?

3.  How do false teachings affect believers, and how can sound doctrine protect against this?

4.  How has your understanding of doctrine changed through this study?

5.  Who in your life could benefit from learning these foundational teachings, and how can you begin discipling them?

## Group Discussion Questions (20-30 minutes)

1.  **Opening Question (Icebreaker)** - *“What is one teaching or belief that had a major impact on your faith journey?”*

2.  **Digging Deeper** - *“How does doctrine shape not only what we believe, but how we live?”*

3.  **Practical Application** - *“What steps can we take to ensure we’re growing in sound doctrine while guarding against false teaching?*

# Chapter 2 - God-Eternal Creator and Loving Father

It is crucial that we establish a firm foundation in the knowledge of who God is. We can come to understand by studying:

- His desires, intents, and actions,

- His nature, character, and attributes,

- What is said of Him and what He says of Himself.

Scripture reveals God’s characteristics uniquely, with each facet deepening our understanding of the unchanging, eternal God.

Summarizing God in one descriptor is challenging, if not impossible, as He is too vast and multifaceted for simplification. While we can, and will, make great efforts to explain, describe, and depict God, ultimately like art and music, He must be experienced to be understood.

When a person listens to music, they describe it by expressing how it moves them-how it resonates within, beyond just the notes and rhythms. A melody might carry warmth or intensity, with a rhythm that pulses like a heartbeat or crescendos that create a sense of exhilaration or longing. Music can soothe like a gentle wave or electrify like a storm, lifting the listener’s spirits, grounding their thoughts, or transporting them to another place entirely. This experience is far more than the sequence of sounds; it is the soul of the music that lingers, captured in how it shapes the listener’s emotions and personal journey.

Similarly, a viewer of a painting looks beyond the colors or brushstrokes, exploring what these elements embody. A landscape might evoke a serene escape, while an abstract piece can stir curiosity or introspection. Certain shades can bring warmth, while a composition may create a sense of calm, chaos, or beauty. The painting becomes a living experience, provoking a shift in perspective or a deep emotional reaction that words alone can’t capture. Both music and art, then, transform into more than their physical forms-they become encounters that touch something essential, changing the listener or viewer in profound and personal ways.

## Exploring God Through the Old Testament

### Genesis: The God Who Dwells With His Creation

A central theme in the narrative of God is His **desire to dwell** with His creation. This is clear in the stories throughout Genesis. Across its fifty chapters and genealogies from Adam (the first man) through Joseph (that last major figure in the book of Genesis, the last significant leader of God’s people before Moses), there is a period of approximately ~2,400 years. We can see through these stories the consistency of God’s goodness despite man’s wickedness.

In Genesis, God establishes relationship with select individuals.

1.  **Adam and Eve** - The first humans, created by God and placed in the Garden of Eden. God walks and talks with them, giving them instructions and establishing a relationship of mutual love and obedience. However, this relationship is impacted by their disobedience in eating from the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil (Genesis 1-3).

2.  **Cain** - Although Cain ultimately rebels by murdering his brother Abel, God speaks directly to him, warning him about the danger of sin and offering him guidance before the murder. Even after Cain’s punishment, God marks him to protect him from harm (Genesis 4).

3.  **Noah** - God establishes a unique relationship with Noah, a righteous man, in a time of widespread corruption. God commands Noah to build an ark to save his family and the animals from the Flood. Afterward, God makes a covenant with Noah, promising never again to destroy the earth with a flood and establishing the rainbow as a sign of this covenant (Genesis 6-9).

4.  **Abraham (Abram)** - One of the most significant relationships in Genesis, God calls Abram to leave his homeland and promises to make him the father of a great nation. God establishes a covenant with Abram, promising land, descendants, and blessings for all nations through him. Abram’s faith and obedience become central themes, and his name is changed to Abraham as part of the covenant (Genesis 12, 15, 17, 22).

5.  **Hagar** - An Egyptian servant of Sarah, Hagar encounters God in the wilderness after being mistreated. God comforts her, promises her a future for her son Ishmael, and instructs her to return to Abraham and Sarah. Hagar refers to God as “El Roi” (“The God Who Sees Me”), recognizing His care for her (Genesis 16).

6.  **Isaac** - The son of Abraham and Sarah, Isaac inherits the covenant promises made to his father. God reaffirms these promises directly to Isaac, including the promise of descendants and blessings. God also guides Isaac during a famine, protecting him and establishing the covenant relationship (Genesis 26).

7.  **Rebekah** - Although her relationship with God is indirect, God answers Rebekah’s prayer about her difficult pregnancy, revealing that her twins, Jacob and Esau, will become two nations with distinct destinies (Genesis 25).

8.  **Jacob** - God establishes a personal relationship with Jacob, appearing to him in a dream and confirming the covenant promises of land, descendants, and blessings. Jacob encounters God several times, including the famous wrestling match where God changes his name to Israel, symbolizing his transformation and the establishment of Israel as a people (Genesis 28, 32, 35).

9.  **Joseph** - Although Joseph’s relationship with God isn’t based on direct appearances or covenants like those of Abraham or Jacob, God is deeply involved in his life. Through dreams, divine favor, and providence, God guides Joseph’s journey from being sold into slavery to becoming a powerful leader in Egypt. Joseph’s faith in God’s sovereignty allows him to forgive his brothers, recognizing that God used his suffering for good (Genesis 37, 39-50).

Throughout this narrative God, who wants to dwell with His creation, has to settle for dwelling with select individuals, and ultimately through covenant with Abraham establishes a people (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="17" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 17:7</span>).

### Exodus: The God Who Dwells With His People

God’s desire to dwell with His people is also clearly seen in the second book of the Bible, Exodus. In the book of the exodus the people of Israel, who had lived for a period of time in relative peace and harmony in Egypt, are now being oppressed by a Pharaoh who did not know of Joseph and feared the Hebrews (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="1" data-verses="6-8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 1:6-8</span>). God’s people lived in Egypt for roughly ~400 years (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="12" data-verses="40-41" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 12:40-41</span>), of which the later portion of that was spent living as slaves (there is not a universal consensus on precisely how long Israel was in bondage to Egypt).

In the powerful presence of the burning bush that was not consumed, God reveals Himself to a Hebrew-born, Egyptian-raised, murderer in exile, who is tending to his father-in-law’s flock in the desert, Moses.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="3" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Exodus 3:14</u>** (NKJV) *And God said to Moses, "I AM WHO I AM." And He said, "Thus you shall say to the children of Israel, 'I AM has sent me to you.' "*

</span>


The phrase **“I AM THAT I AM”** (Ehyeh-Asher-Ehyeh) is one of the most profound self-revelations in Scripture. When Moses asks for God’s name, God responds with this phrase, which can be translated as "I Am Who I Am," "I Will Be Who I Will Be," or simply "I Am." This declaration on the surface is a bit confusing but holds deep meaning. Could you imagine introducing yourself to someone new as ‘I am’? When God reveals Himself as ‘I AM THAT I AM’ He is attesting to His nature, His eternal presence, His sovereignty. ‘I am’ is infinite in meaning and purpose.

God uses Moses to reveal Himself to Pharaoh:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="7" data-verses="16" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Exodus 7:16</u>** (NKJV) *And you shall say to him, 'The LORD God of the Hebrews has sent me to you, saying, "Let My people go, that they may serve Me in the wilderness"; but indeed, until now you would not hear!*

</span>


Moses tells Pharaoh, God is the ‘I AM’ and that God demands the Israelites be released so that they may worship Him in the wilderness.

Plagues and misery ensue, but ultimately Pharaoh relents and lets the Israelites leave.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="12" data-verses="31" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Exodus 12:31</u>** (NKJV) *Then he called for Moses and Aaron by night, and said, "Rise, go out from among my people, both you and the children of Israel. And go, serve the LORD as you have said.*

</span>


In the wilderness God miraculously delivers His people from the vengeful Egyptian army (Exodus 14), provides protection from the sun and a pillar of fire to keep them warm in the cool night of the desert (Exodus 13), food from heaven (Exodus 16), and water to drink (Exodus 17).

God brings His people to Mount Sinai where God commands Moses to consecrate the people and gives them the law (including the 10 commandments) and establishes His covenant with them (the old covenant) (Exodus 19, 20, 24). This covenant (the Mosaic covenant) builds upon the Abrahamic covenant and lasts from Moses to Jesus.

Despite building a covenant with His people, the relationship is one of a loving and jealous God pursuing and abiding with very stubborn and ungrateful people (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="32" data-verses="9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 32:9</span>). Ignoring God's miraculous intervention, they keep a rebellious attitude for large parts of the text. God even brings them to the promised land and tells them they can inherit it (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="33" data-verses="3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 33:3</span>), but in fear and disobedience they ignore the supernatural deliverance that God has given them, and instead must wander the desert for 40 years until almost everyone from that generation had died (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Numbers" data-chapter="14" data-verses="33-34" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Numbers 14:33-34</span>).

### The Rest of the Old Testament

In the ~1,400 years between the death of Moses and the birth of Christ the Israelites experienced a recurring cycle of devotion which brings God's favor and blessings, followed by rebellion, God’s judgment, their subsequent repentance, and ultimately God’s deliverance. This pattern is particularly evident in the books of **Judges, Kings, and Chronicles**, which recount cycles where the people turn away from God, often by worshiping other gods, resulting in periods of oppression or exile. In response to their cries, God repeatedly raised up judges, prophets, or leaders to deliver them.

Throughout these years, despite the Israelites' frequent failures to uphold their covenant with Him, God remains faithful, showing both justice and mercy. He continuously rescues and forgives His people, demonstrating His enduring commitment and love.

As we see in Scripture, God reveals Himself through actions, relationships, and even names that speak to His character. Each name represents a specific aspect of His total character. These names were given as part of a narrative of how God revealed Himself to His people. For your personal study a reference table is included in the Appendix: *Table 10 - Old Testament Names of God.* Read through these names and study the scripture references. Each reference is a story that depicts precisely how God saved, delivered, and rescued His people.

Also included in the Appendix is a further derived set of attributes describing His nature, descriptions of His actions and intents: *Table 11 - Attributes of God.* These attributes, while slightly more abstract than the names of God, are no less authentic!

These names help us understand God-Father, Son, and Holy Spirit (the **Trinity**)-working together as one. In our continued study we will examine names and attributes of Jesus and the Holy Spirit in more depth.

God’s character and actions are fully expressed in the unity of Father, Son, and Spirit. The scriptures teach that God is one in essence, yet exists as three distinct persons: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit. Scripture affirms God’s oneness, stating, “beside Him there is no God” (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="45" data-verses="5" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Isaiah 45:5</span>). However, the Godhead is understood as a **compound unity**-three distinct persons who cooperate with a singular mind and purpose, united in the truest sense. Each person of the Trinity has a unique role: the **Father creates**, the **Son redeems**, and the **Holy Spirit sanctifies**. The term “Trinity” does not appear in the Bible; it was developed as a theological expression to describe this concept, which is revealed through Scripture.

The Trinity can be illustrated by examples in nature, which help clarify this idea of unity in diversity. Like water, which can be liquid, ice, or vapor but remains H₂O, each person of the Trinity is fully God yet distinct in personhood. Or consider a triangle, with three sides and three angles, forming one complete shape; if one side is removed, it ceases to be a triangle. Similarly, **God’s nature is incomplete without all three persons**, who work together in perfect unity.

- **God the Father**: He is the creator and sustainer of all things, often understood as the initiator in the Godhead. His love for humanity is central to the biblical story, exemplified in <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="3" data-verses="16" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 3:16</span>. In our study we will examine God the father as the loving father and righteous judge, who made a path for our redemption.

- **God the Son**: Co-existent and co-eternal with the Father, Jesus took on human nature, being conceived by the Holy Spirit and born of the virgin Mary. He lived a sinless life, died to redeem humanity, and rose again, defeating sin and death. He now sits at the Father’s right hand, interceding for us (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="1" data-verses="1-3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 1:1-3</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="1" data-verses="23" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 1:23</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Timothy" data-chapter="2" data-verses="5" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Timothy 2:5</span>). In our study we will examine Jesus as the perfect atoning sacrifice, the resurrected Lord, and head of the church.

- **God the Holy Spirit**: The Holy Spirit, co-existent and equal with the Father and the Son, is sent by the Father through the Son to convict the world of sin and prepare believers as the Bride of Christ. He empowers, comforts, and sanctifies those who are in Christ (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="14" data-verses="26" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 14:26</span>; 15:26; 16:8). In our study we will examine the Holy Spirit as the comforter, given to us by God to equip, empower, sanctify, and abide with us.

Through these distinct persons, God’s triune nature is revealed as a perfect union in purpose, will, and love, each person playing a vital role in the divine mission of creation, redemption, and sanctification. The Trinity invites believers to experience God fully, both as a loving Father, a redeeming Savior, and a transforming Spirit.

## Conclusion

We can be uniquely known by our names: husband, father, son, brother, friend. Each of these a declaration of who we are in relation to someone else. We can also be known by our attributes: faithful, patient, kind, intelligent, funny. Likewise, we can know God by His attributes and names- He is our Healer, Provider, Shepherd, and Redeemer. However, as labels and words are only a small part of us, for ourselves to be truly known by others, we must be experienced. Even more so with God. To truly and fully know Him we must experience Him.

God’s names reveal His character, but personal encounters deepen our understanding of what these names signify. For instance, “God my Healer” takes on a richer, more profound meaning when we understand the depths of illness, desperation, or helplessness from which God rescues. Knowing Him as our Provider is transformed when we have felt true need and found Him faithful.

Scriptures offer a context to these encounters, showing us God’s faithfulness to His people across time and circumstance, reinforcing how His names and attributes are not mere titles but realities He brings to life, experienced in the lives of individuals. It is through this interplay of personal experience and the scriptural narrative that we come to know God-not just as a distant description but as a present, active relationship in every season of our lives.

## Personal Reflection Questions 

1.  What attribute of God resonates most with you right now? Why?

2.  How does knowing God as “I AM” (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="3" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 3:14</span>) change the way you approach Him in prayer?

3.  Which of God’s names speaks most to your current situation (e.g., Yahweh-Jireh, Yahweh-Rapha)? How has He shown Himself in this way to you?

4.  How does understanding God’s faithfulness in the Old Testament help you trust Him in areas of uncertainty today?

5.  What does the Trinity reveal about God’s relational nature, and how does it impact your relationship with Him?

## Group Discussion Questions (20-30 minutes)

1.  **Opening Question (Icebreaker):** *Which of God’s names or attributes stood out to you the most during this chapter? Why?*

2.  **Exploring Scripture:** *Looking at <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="3" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 3:14</span> and <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="22" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 22:14</span>, how do God’s names reflect His character and actions in those stories?*

3.  **Practical Application:** *How can understanding God’s attributes (e.g., faithful, merciful, sovereign) impact the way we respond to challenges in life?*

4.  **The Trinity Discussion:** *How does the Trinity demonstrate unity and purpose? How can this model influence relationships in the church?*

5.  **Reflection:** *Where in your life do you need to see more of God’s provision, healing, or peace? How can the group pray for you in this area?*

# Chapter 3 - Sin and Redemption

Scripture tells us many stories about sin-its causes, its consequences, and how God responds to it. To sin is to fall short of God’s standard, or ‘miss the mark,’ a term from archery describing an archer’s failure to hit the target.

Both Old and New Testaments use various terms for sin, each highlighting aspects like its type, severity, intent, victim, nature, and required punishment. There are several broad categorizations that the scriptures use to describe sin:

- Sin against God, sin against man, sin against self

- Intentional sin, unintentional sin

- Sin of a single moment, sin habitually committed

- Sin requiring severe punishment like death, sin requiring financial restitution

In our modern criminal justice systems, we have similar distinctions: degrees of crime, premeditated or crimes of passion, crimes requiring financial restitution, or incarceration, or even capital punishment. An act might be both a crime and a sin, but crime is man's categorization, sin is God’s.

The various Greek and Hebrew words for sin have a high level of overlap. All sin is broadly described under the umbrella term ‘missing the mark’, ***Hamartia*** in Greek, and ***Chata’*** in Hebrew. This umbrella term is used for the most severe sins, adultery, murder, but also less severe sins, like showing partiality to wealthy people in our church gatherings (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="James" data-chapter="2" data-verses="9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">James 2:9</span>).

Here are some words the Scriptures use for sin. They help us understand how serious sin is-and how deeply we need God's grace.

## Sin has Consequences

Every sin brings consequences-sometimes immediate, sometimes delayed, but always real.

Let's consider Cain’s murder of his brother Able in Genesis 4.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="4" data-verses="1-16" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Genesis 4:1-16</u>** (NKJV)
>
>***1** Now Adam knew Eve his wife, and she conceived and bore Cain, and said, "I have acquired a man from the LORD."*
>
>***2** Then she bore again, this time his brother Abel. Now Abel was a keeper of sheep, but Cain was a tiller of the ground.*
>
>***3** And in the process of time it came to pass that Cain brought an offering of the fruit of the ground to the LORD.*
>
>***4** Abel also brought of the firstborn of his flock and of their fat. And the LORD respected Abel and his offering,*
>
>***5** but He did not respect Cain and his offering. And Cain very angry, and his countenance fell.*
>
>***6** So the LORD said to Cain, "Why are you angry? And why has your countenance fallen?*
>
>***7** If you do well, will you not be accepted? And if you do not do well, sin lies at the door. And its desire is for you, but you should rule over it."*
>
>***8** Now Cain talked with Abel his brother; and it came to pass, when they were in the field, that Cain rose up against Abel his brother and killed him.*
>
>***9** Then the LORD said to Cain, "Where is Abel your brother?" He said, "I do not know. Am I my brother's keeper?"*
>
>***10** And He said, "What have you done? The voice of your brother's blood cries out to Me from the ground.*
>
>***11** So now you are cursed from the earth, which has opened its mouth to receive your brother's blood from your hand.*
>
>***12** When you till the ground, it shall no longer yield its strength to you. A fugitive and a vagabond you shall be on the earth."*
>
>***13** And Cain said to the LORD, "My punishment is greater than I can bear!*
>
>***14** Surely You have driven me out this day from the face of the ground; I shall be hidden from Your face; I shall be a fugitive and a vagabond on the earth, and it will happen that anyone who finds me will kill me."*
>
>***15** And the LORD said to him, "Therefore, whoever kills Cain, vengeance shall be taken on him sevenfold." And the LORD set a mark on Cain, lest anyone finding him should kill him.*
>
>***16** Then Cain went out from the presence of the LORD and dwelt in the land of Nod on the east of Eden.*

</span>


Where did Cain miss the mark? (in chronological order)

1.  He failed to offer a proper sacrifice.

2.  He ignored the warning from Jehovah (God), that sin was waiting to devour him.

3.  He murdered his brother.

4.  He lied to God about his act of murder.

What were the consequences of Cain’s actions?

- His sacrifice was not accepted.

- He felt miserable about the state of his sacrifice.

- He was devoured by sin.

- His brother was dead, his parents lost their child.

- God cursed the ground against Cain, he had to live as a vagabond.

- Cain had to endure his punishment; he would not get relief.

- Cain was marked by God, so that he would not be killed by others, but others knew he was a murderer. He would be socially isolated.

- Cain left the presence of God; the scriptures do not mention Cain ever being with God again.

Pretty severe! Cain’s sin carried a hefty price for himself, for Able, for his parents, and for his own future family and lineage.

God is a holy (pure, innocent, blameless) God. When man sins, a person separates himself from God, and the effect of our sin is spiritual death, and quite possibly natural death as well.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="59" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Isaiah 59:2</u>** (NKJV) *But your iniquities have separated you from your God; And your sins have hidden His face from you, So that He will not hear.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Romans" data-chapter="6" data-verses="23" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Romans 6:23</u>** (NKJV) *For the wages of sin is death, but the gift of God is eternal life in Christ Jesus our Lord.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Galatians" data-chapter="6" data-verses="7-8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Galatians 6:7-8</u>** (NKJV) **7** *Do not be deceived, God is not mocked; for whatever a man sows, that he will also reap. **8** For he who sows to his flesh will of the flesh reap corruption, but he who sows to the Spirit will of the Spirit reap everlasting life.*

</span>


God cannot have fellowship with unrighteousness. The pain and effect of sin is that it naturally separates us from our relationship with God.

### Judgment - Now and Eternal

The scriptures declare that there will be final judgement for every man according to his deed. This judgement, called the ***Great White Throne Judgement,** happens* after the **resurrection of the dead to condemnation** (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="5" data-verses="28-29" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 5:28-29</span>), and is for everyone whose name is not in the ***Lamb’s Book of Life**.* <u>This judgement is not for the believer, but for the unbeliever</u>, believers face the ***Judgement Seat of Christ*** (read the final chapter for more details). The scriptures say God will pour out His wrath upon those who have broken His laws; His judgment will be eternal separation and misery. Jesus says in <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="5" data-verses="26-27" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 5:26-27</span> that the Father has given Him authority to carry out this judgement.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="20" data-verses="12-13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Revelation 20:12-13</u>** (NKJV) ***12** And I saw the dead, small and great, standing before God, and books were opened… **13** … And they were judged, each one according to his works.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="12" data-verses="36-37" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Matthew 12:36-37</u>** (NKJV) ***36** But I say to you that for every idle word men may speak, they will give account of it in the day of judgment.* ***37** For by your words you will be justified, and by your words you will be condemned."*

</span>


We will likely face the consequence of sin in our natural lives. We may perhaps feel that we’ve somehow escaped from some portion of the punishment, but there will be no escape man can make on his own accord against God’s judgment after our natural death.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Hebrews" data-chapter="9" data-verses="27" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Hebrews 9:27</u>** (NKJV) * And as it is appointed for men to die once, but after this the judgment,*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Ecclesiastes" data-chapter="12" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Ecclesiastes 12:14</u>** (NKJV) *For God will bring every work into judgment, Including every secret thing, Whether good or evil.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Romans" data-chapter="2" data-verses="5-6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Romans 2:5-6</u>** (NKJV) ***5** But in accordance with your hardness and your impenitent heart you are treasuring up for yourself wrath in the day of wrath and revelation of the righteous judgment of God, **6** who "WILL RENDER TO EACH ONE ACCORDING TO HIS DEEDS":*

</span>


Knowledge of judgment isn’t meant to leave us in fear but to awaken us to the seriousness of sin and the depth of God’s mercy. It is God’s loving call to repentance-a call to turn from sin and receive the forgiveness and restoration He freely offers through Christ.

Even in the midst of judgment, God’s heart for redemption shines through. From the first sacrifice in Genesis, where He covered Adam and Eve’s shame with animal skins, to the intricate sacrificial system He established for Israel, we see a pattern of grace woven into God’s plan. These sacrifices were not the final solution but foreshadowed the ultimate sacrifice to come, Jesus Christ, the Lamb of God, who takes away the sin of the world. As we examine these Old Testament sacrifices, we begin to see how God prepared His people to understand the cost of sin and the hope of restoration.

## Sin Requires Sacrifice

In the story of Adam and Eve, God kills animals and makes clothes for them from their skins (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="3" data-verses="20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 3:20</span>). This was the first shedding of blood to cover the consequence of sin. There are a few other similar accounts in the book of Genesis, but God formally institutes a method of sacrifice for sin in the book of Leviticus.
