 Here are a few examples to help understand the difference between power and authority.

Example 1: At work, an employee may have the talents and skills to conduct a sale of the company's assets (power). This power is lawful if they also have the associated permission (authority) from the company’s owners. Without this authority, selling the company's assets is theft.

Example 2: A police officer is given special training in weapons and hand-to-hand combat to learn how to apprehend and physically compel a criminal (power). Police officers have been given the job responsibility backed up by laws (authority) to enforce criminal statutes.

## What is Jesus’ Authority?

As Jesus said in Matthew, ‘All authority’ has been given. Let's look at precisely what that authority entails:

- Over Heaven and Earth

- Over life and death

- Over sin and forgiveness

- Over demons and spiritual forces

- Over sickness and disease

- To judge humanity

- Over the Church

- Over creation and nature

- Over death, Hell, and the grave

- To empower believers

### Authority Over Heaven and Earth


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Matthew 28:18</u>** (NKJV) *And Jesus came and spoke to them, saying, "All authority has been given to Me in heaven and on earth.*

</span>


Christ’s authority extends to everything in creation, including the physical and spiritual realms. This authority establishes His sovereign rule as King of kings and Lord of lords (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="19" data-verses="16" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 19:16</span>). It forms the foundation for the Great Commission, empowering believers to spread the Gospel.

### Authority Over Life and Death


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="John" data-chapter="10" data-verses="17-18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>John 10:17-18</u>** (NKJV) **17** *Therefore My Father loves Me, because I lay down My life that I may take it again. **18** No one takes it from Me, but I lay it down of Myself. I have power to lay it down, and I have power to take it again. This command I have received from My Father.*

</span>


Jesus has complete control over life and death, demonstrated in His death and resurrection. He holds the key to eternal life, offering victory over death and fear for those who believe (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Timothy" data-chapter="1" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Timothy 1:10</span>).

### Authority Over Sin and Forgiveness


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="2" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Mark 2:10</u>** (NKJV) *But that you may know that the Son of Man has power on earth to forgive sins…*

</span>


Christ has the authority to forgive sins, reconciling humanity to God through His atoning sacrifice. This authority demonstrates His divine nature and role as the Savior (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 John" data-chapter="2" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 John 2:2</span>).

### Authority Over Demons and Spiritual Forces


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Luke" data-chapter="10" data-verses="19" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Luke 10:19</u>** (NKJV) * Behold, I give you the authority to trample on serpents and scorpions, and over all the power of the enemy, and nothing shall by any means hurt you.*

</span>


Jesus commands demons and spiritual powers, enforcing His victory over Satan (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 John" data-chapter="3" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 John 3:8</span>). He delegates this authority to believers, enabling them to cast out demons and minister deliverance (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="16" data-verses="17" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Mark 16:17</span>).

### Authority Over Sickness and Disease


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="8" data-verses="16-17" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Matthew 8:16-17</u> (NKJV):** **16** *When evening had come, they brought to Him many who were demon-possessed. And He cast out the spirits with a word, and healed all who were sick, **17** that it might be fulfilled which was spoken by Isaiah the prophet, saying: "HE HIMSELF TOOK OUR INFIRMITIES AND BORE OUR SICKNESSES."*

</span>


Jesus healed all types of diseases, fulfilling prophecy and demonstrating His authority over both sickness and spiritual affliction. Jesus used healing as a sign of His authority to forgive sin, showing that physical and spiritual restoration are both within His command.

### Authority to Judge All Humanity


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="John" data-chapter="5" data-verses="22-23" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>John 5:22-23</u>** (NKJV) **22** *For the Father judges no one, but has committed all judgment to the Son, **23** that all should honor the Son just as they honor the Father. He who does not honor the Son does not honor the Father who sent Him.*

</span>


Christ is the appointed judge over all people, both the living and the dead (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="10" data-verses="42" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Acts 10:42</span>). His judgments are righteous, ensuring that evil is punished, and faithfulness is rewarded. Believers face the Judgment Seat of Christ for rewards (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Corinthians" data-chapter="5" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Corinthians 5:10</span>), while unbelievers face the Great White Throne Judgment for eternal separation (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="20" data-verses="11-15" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 20:11-15</span>).

### Authority Over the Church


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Ephesians" data-chapter="1" data-verses="22-23" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Ephesians 1:22-23</u>** (NKJV) **22** *And He put all things under His feet, and gave Him to be head over all things to the church, **23** which is His body, the fullness of Him who fills all in all.*

</span>


Christ is the head of the Church, guiding and equipping believers through His Word and Spirit. He delegates spiritual gifts and appoints leaders to nurture, protect, and equip the Church (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Ephesians" data-chapter="4" data-verses="11-13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Ephesians 4:11-13</span>).

### Authority Over Creation and Nature


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Colossians" data-chapter="1" data-verses="16-17" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Colossians 1:16-17</u>** (NKJV) ***16** For by Him all things were created that are in heaven and that are on earth, visible and invisible … All things were created through Him and for Him.* ***17** And He is before all things, and in Him all things consist.*

</span>


Jesus is the Creator and Sustainer of the universe, demonstrating sovereignty over all natural forces. He commands storms to be still (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="4" data-verses="39" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Mark 4:39</span>) and multiplies food, showing His power over nature.

### Authority Over Death, Hell, and the Grave


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="1" data-verses="18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Revelation 1:18</u>** (NKJV)* I am He who lives, and was dead, and behold, I am alive forevermore. Amen. And I have the keys of Hades and of Death.*

</span>


Christ has victory over death, hell, and the grave, symbolized by His possession of the keys. This guarantees eternal life for believers and establishes His sovereignty over the afterlife (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Corinthians" data-chapter="15" data-verses="54-57" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Corinthians 15:54-57</span>).

### Authority to Empower Believers


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="1" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Acts 1:8</u>** *(NKJV) But you shall receive power when the Holy Spirit has come upon you; and you shall be witnesses to Me in Jerusalem, and in all Judea and Samaria, and to the end of the earth.*

</span>


Christ empowers believers through the Holy Spirit to proclaim the Gospel, perform miracles, and minister effectively. Believers act under His authority while relying on the Holy Spirit’s power to fulfill their mission (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="18-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 28:18-20</span>).

## The Source of Jesus’ Authority

Now that we have looked at precisely what authority is we need to know who the source of that authority is. Jesus’ authority is both divine and delegated. It originates from God the Father, is affirmed through His death and resurrection, and operates in perfect harmony with the Father’s will. Though absolute, His authority respects human free will, demonstrating God’s love rather than forceful control.

Jesus’ authority is rooted in His divine identity as the Son of God and is granted by the Father. The Father entrusted all authority to Christ, including judgment and rule over heaven and earth (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="11" data-verses="27" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 11:27</span>). Jesus’ authority reflects the unity and love within the Trinity, where the Son carries out the Father’s plan perfectly (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="3" data-verses="35" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 3:35</span>).

While Jesus had inherent authority as God’s Son, His victory over sin, death, and hell was fully established through His death and resurrection. Christ’s obedience in His sacrifice led to His exaltation (seated at the right hand of the Father), securing His authority over all creation. By conquering death, Christ gained absolute dominion over life and eternity.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="1" data-verses="18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Revelation 1:18</u>** (NKJV) * I am He who lives, and was dead, and behold, I am alive forevermore. Amen. And I have the keys of Hades and of Death.*

</span>


Even though Jesus has all authority, He uses it in full agreement with the Father’s heart and purpose:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="John" data-chapter="5" data-verses="19" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>John 5:19</u>** (NKJV) *Then Jesus answered and said to them, "Most assuredly, I say to you, the Son can do nothing of Himself, but what He sees the Father do; for whatever He does, the Son also does in like manner.*

</span>


Jesus does not act independently; instead, He mirrors the Father’s actions, ensuring His authority is always just and righteous. Even His words and teachings reflect the father’s intent, emphasizing their unity in purpose (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="12" data-verses="49" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 12:49</span>).

Jesus never forces anyone to obey. He respects our freedom and gently invites us to follow Him.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="3" data-verses="20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Revelation 3:20</u>** (NKJV) *Behold, I stand at the door and knock. If anyone hears My voice and opens the door, I will come in to him and dine with him, and he with Me.*

</span>


Christ’s authority works through love and grace to restore broken lives and empower believers. Christ calls, but does not force obedience, demonstrating love instead of domination. While Jesus does not override free will, He allows people to experience the consequences of their choices:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Galatians" data-chapter="6" data-verses="7-8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Galatians 6:7-8</u>** (NKJV) **7** *Do not be deceived, God is not mocked; for whatever a man sows, that he will also reap.* ***8** For he who sows to his flesh will of the flesh reap corruption, but he who sows to the Spirit will of the Spirit reap everlasting life.*

</span>


## Jesus Delegated Certain Authority to His Followers

Jesus delegated specific areas of authority to believers. This authority is relational. We only have this authority in relationship to Jesus. We have no authority apart from our relationship with Jesus. Likewise, the power we have been given by the Holy Spirit is only through our relationship with Him. Apart from Him, we can do nothing.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="John" data-chapter="14" data-verses="12-14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>John 14:12-14</u>** (NKJV) **12** *"Most assuredly, I say to you, he who believes in Me, the works that I do he will do also; and greater works than these he will do, because I go to My Father.* ***13** And whatever you ask in My name, that I will do, that the Father may be glorified in the Son.* ***14** If you ask anything in My name, I will do it.*

</span>


Jesus said these greater works were tied to His name (His rightful authority), not ours! These works are to be done to glorify God in the Son. The scriptures say we must ask, and what we ask for must be in line with God’s will; our asks of God are not for personal enrichment, misuse, or abuse, but for the purpose of kingdom of God:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="James" data-chapter="4" data-verses="2-3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>James 4:2-3</u>** (NKJV) **2** … Yet you do not have because you do not ask **3** … You ask and do not receive, because you ask amiss,*

</span>


To know the will of God, renew your mind! Paul says renewing the mind and being transformed is key to knowing the good, acceptable, and perfect will of God (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Romans" data-chapter="12" data-verses="1-2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Romans 12:1-2</span>).

When we ask, we are to ask in faith (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="11" data-verses="24" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Mark 11:24</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="21" data-verses="22" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 21:22</span>). Reminding ourselves that **faith is not magic but is persuasion and trust**. Jesus as the source of complete and perfect truth is able to persuade us of His will and His ability to deliver on His promises (Hebrews 11).


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="16" data-verses="15-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Mark 16:15-20</u>** (NKJV)
>
>***15** And He said to them, "Go into all the world and preach the gospel to every creature.*
>
>***16** He who believes and is baptized will be saved; but he who does not believe will be condemned.*
>
>***17** And these signs will follow those who believe: In My name they will cast out demons; they will speak with new tongues;*
>
>***18** they will take up serpents; and if they drink anything deadly, it will by no means hurt them; they will lay hands on the sick, and they will recover."*
>
>***19** So then, after the Lord had spoken to them, He was received up into heaven, and sat down at the right hand of God.*
>
>***20** And they went out and preached everywhere, the Lord working with them and confirming the word through the accompanying signs. Amen.*

</span>


Jesus gave us His authority to:

- **Preach the Gospel & Baptize Believers** - This is Christ’s authority to build His church and make disciples (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="19-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 28:19-20</span>). Boldly proclaiming the message of reconciliation and forgiveness of sins. This is the authority to lead in the Body of Christ as faithful under shepherds and servants and to practice discipline (train, correct, rebuke) in our gatherings (1 Peter 5).

- **Cast Out Devils** - This is Christ's authority over the works of the devil and evil spirits. We have the authority to resist the devil individually and to bring deliverance to those impacted and oppressed by him (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Luke" data-chapter="10" data-verses="19" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Luke 10:19</span>).

- **Speak with New Tongues** - This is Christ’s promise of supernatural empowerment by the manifestation of the Holy Spirit.

- **Protection from Serpents and from Poison** - This is Christ's authority and provision to keep us safe from harms and injury in our work. We are not to put God to a foolish test (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="4" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 4:7</span>).

- **Heal the Sick** - This is Christ’s authority to heal the sick, physically, emotionally, and spiritually (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="James" data-chapter="5" data-verses="14-15" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">James 5:14-15</span>). To bind up the broken hearted, open the (spiritual and physical) eyes of the blind, to set at liberty those who are held in captivity (Isaiah 61 / Luke 4).

This authority is not given for personal gain but as a tool to advance God’s Kingdom, bring healing, and set people free. It requires humility, dependence on the Holy Spirit, and alignment with God’s will. As we step into this authority, we do so in faith, knowing that all power ultimately belongs to Christ (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 28:18</span>).

The authority we’ve received from Christ is put into operation through our expression. Many times, this expression is verbalized in prayer, or in speaking, but in many cases is also expressed through the laying on of hands.

## Laying on of Hands

The laying on of hands is a significant biblical practice used throughout both the Old Testament and New Testament to symbolize blessing, commissioning, healing, and the impartation of spiritual authority or gifts. It is more than just a physical act-it represents a spiritual connection and transfer under the direction of God’s authority.

In the Old Testament, the laying on of hands was often associated with blessing and dedication. For example, Jacob blessed his grandchildren, Ephraim, and Manasseh, by laying his hands on them (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="48" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 48:14</span>). Similarly, Moses laid hands on Joshua to commission him as his successor, imparting leadership authority and wisdom (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Numbers" data-chapter="27" data-verses="18-23" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Numbers 27:18-23</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Deuteronomy" data-chapter="34" data-verses="9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Deuteronomy 34:9</span>). This act publicly signified God’s selection and empowerment for the task ahead.

In the New Testament, the laying on of hands is closely tied to the ministry of Jesus and the early church. Jesus regularly laid hands on the sick to heal them (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="6" data-verses="5" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Mark 6:5</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Luke" data-chapter="4" data-verses="40" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Luke 4:40</span>), demonstrating both compassion and authority over sickness and disease. The apostles continued this practice, using it to impart the Holy Spirit (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="8" data-verses="17" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Acts 8:17</span>), spiritual gifts (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Timothy" data-chapter="4" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Timothy 4:14</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Timothy" data-chapter="1" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Timothy 1:6</span>), and blessings to believers. It also played a role in commissioning leaders and missionaries for ministry (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="13" data-verses="3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Acts 13:3</span>).

The laying on of hands symbolizes an act of faith and obedience to God’s Word. It is not a mechanical ritual, but a spiritual practice rooted in dependence on the Holy Spirit. For example, in <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="6" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Acts 6:6</span>, the apostles laid hands on those selected to serve as deacons, committing them to God’s service and publicly acknowledging their calling. In <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="19" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Acts 19:6</span>, Paul laid hands on believers, and they received the Holy Spirit, demonstrating its role in spiritual empowerment.

However, the Bible also gives warnings about the practice:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="1 Timothy" data-chapter="5" data-verses="22" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>1 Timothy 5:22</u>** (NKJV) *Do not lay hands on anyone hastily, nor share in other people's sins; keep yourself pure.*

</span>


This highlights the importance of discernment and spiritual maturity when appointing individuals to leadership roles. As we studied in the chapter on the church, leaders must be tested and proven to avoid promoting unqualified or immature believers who could bring harm to the Body of Christ.

A few more guidelines for laying on of hands:

1.  Contextually appropriate - the person who is having hands laid on them should be a willing participant.

2.  Physically appropriate - laying on of hands does not involve sexual or sensual touch. In practice laying a hand on a shoulder, hand, or forehead are generally accepted places to touch (with permission) in the western world.

3.  Culturally appropriate - in some cultures it is not appropriate for men and women to have touch unless they are related by blood or marriage.

The laying on of hands continues to be a powerful expression of faith and obedience in the Church today. It serves as a means of blessing, healing, commissioning, and empowerment, always pointing to the authority of Christ and the work of the Holy Spirit. Whether praying for the sick, sending out missionaries, or commissioning leaders, it is a reminder that believers operate not in their own power but under the authority of Jesus Christ and in alignment with His purposes.

Now that we have studied the authority of Jesus and the authority of the believer, we will discuss the other beings of the spiritual realm, angels and demons.

## Angels and Demons

### Angels

Angels are a significant part of God’s creation, serving as His messengers and agents to carry out His divine purposes. The Bible reveals a spiritual realm inhabited by these beings, who are powerful, intelligent, and obedient to God’s commands. While angels are often unseen, their presence and actions impact human history and spiritual warfare. Understanding their roles, classifications, and activities provides us with deeper insight into God’s plan and the unseen realities that surround us.

The term "angel" itself means "messenger," highlighting their primary function. Angels operate in obedience to God’s will, whether delivering messages, providing protection, executing judgment, or engaging in spiritual battles. They are not objects of worship but serve as examples of obedience and reverence, always pointing back to the glory of God. This chapter explores the nature, classifications, and roles of angels, shedding light on their significance in Scripture and in our lives today.

In the scriptures angels are seen as **messengers**, **servants**, and **warriors**. Angels frequently act as messengers, delivering important announcements, warnings, and encouragements. They reveal God’s plans and provide guidance in times of need (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Luke" data-chapter="1" data-verses="26-38" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Luke 1:26-38</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="2" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 2:13</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="1" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 1:1</span>). Angels are actively involved in protecting, strengthening, and delivering believers. They minister to God’s people in times of distress and provide supernatural aid (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="91" data-verses="11" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 91:11</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="12" data-verses="7-11" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Acts 12:7-11</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="4" data-verses="11" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 4:11</span>). Angels engage in spiritual warfare, battling demonic forces to advance God’s kingdom and defend believers (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Daniel" data-chapter="10" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Daniel 10:13</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="12" data-verses="7-9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 12:7-9</span>).

#### The Nature of Angels

Angels are created beings, brought into existence by God before the foundation of the world. They are distinct from humans, possessing unique characteristics that equip them for their heavenly roles. The Bible teaches that angels are spiritual beings, not bound by physical limitations, yet they can take on visible forms when interacting with humans.

Angels are described as immortal, meaning they do not die or age. They are innumerable, with vast hosts filling the heavenly realms. While they are often referred to with masculine pronouns, Scripture makes it clear that angels are sexless and do not marry or reproduce. These qualities emphasize their purpose as servants of God, entirely devoted to His will and commands. Angels possess remarkable traits that reflect their divine purpose. They are obedient, reverent, wise, and powerful. These qualities equip them to carry out God’s will effectively and faithfully.

<td><strong>Holy</strong></td>
<td>Set apart for God’s purposes, reflecting His holiness.</td>
<td><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="14" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 14:10</span></td>

</table>

Though the Bible highlights several roles and titles for angels, it does not give an exhaustive classification system. However, some angels are identified by specific roles or functions:

- **The Angel of the Lord** - Often considered a pre-incarnate appearance of Christ, appearing with divine authority. (*<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="23" data-verses="20-23" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 23:20-23</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Judges" data-chapter="6" data-verses="11-24" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Judges 6:11-24</span>*)

- **Archangels** - High-ranking angels like Michael, who leads armies, and Gabriel, who delivers divine messages. (*Jude 9; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="12" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 12:7</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Luke" data-chapter="1" data-verses="19-26" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Luke 1:19-26</span>*)

- **Cherubim** - Guardians of God’s glory and presence, such as at Eden and the Ark of the Covenant. (*<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="3" data-verses="24" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 3:24</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="25" data-verses="22" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 25:22</span>*)

- **Seraphim** - Worshipers of God, continually declaring His holiness. (*<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="6" data-verses="1-6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Isaiah 6:1-6</span>*)

- **Elect Angels** - Faithful angels who resisted Satan’s rebellion and remain loyal to God. (*<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Timothy" data-chapter="5" data-verses="21" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Timothy 5:21</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="25" data-verses="41" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 25:41</span>*)

These classifications emphasize the diversity and organization within the angelic realm, yet their primary focus is always fulfilling God’s purposes.

#### Human Interaction with Angels

Humans often encounter angels in Scripture, but we are never instructed to command or pray to them. Instead, angels act according to God’s direction. They minister to believers as **servants of God** (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Hebrews" data-chapter="1" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Hebrews 1:14</span>). Their interactions include protection, guidance, and deliverance, always pointing us back to God’s power and sovereignty.

- **Warnings Against Angel Worship**: Scripture forbids worshiping angels or seeking encounters with them (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Colossians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Colossians 2:18</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="22" data-verses="8-9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 22:8-9</span>).

- **Prayer to God, Not Angels**: Believers should pray to God, who dispatches angels as He wills (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="91" data-verses="11" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 91:11</span>).

- **Encouragement and Trust**: Angels remind us of God’s care, but our faith should rest fully in God, not in angels.

Angels are awe-inspiring beings created to serve God and minister to His people. While their work is often unseen, Scripture provides glimpses into their activity, revealing their faithfulness, strength, and reverence. Angels remind us of God’s provision and protection, but they always point back to His glory. As believers, we are called to trust in God’s care, knowing that angels operate as His servants to fulfill His divine purposes.

### Demons

Just as the study of angels is necessary to understand the spiritual world, so too is the study of demons. Scripture clearly describes their activity, influence, and defeat through Jesus Christ. Recognizing their role in spiritual warfare helps believers stand firm in faith without fear.

#### Origin and Nature of Demons

Demons are fallen angels who followed Satan’s rebellion against God. *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="12" data-verses="7-9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 12:7-9</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="25" data-verses="41" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 25:41</span>).* Satan, formerly known as Lucifer (“light bearer”), an exalted angel, sought to exalt himself above God and was cast out of heaven along with other rebellious angels. *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="14" data-verses="12-15" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Isaiah 14:12-15</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Ezekiel" data-chapter="28" data-verses="12-17" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Ezekiel 28:12-17</span>).*

Demons are spirits without physical bodies but can oppress and torment humans and animals. *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="5" data-verses="11-13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Mark 5:11-13</span>).* They seek to deceive, tempt, oppress, and destroy. *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="10" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 10:10</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Corinthians" data-chapter="11" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Corinthians 11:14</span>). Unlike God,* they have limits, and are not omnipresent (everywhere), omniscient (all knowing), omnipotent (all powerful)*.* Their ultimate defeat is certain, and they will face eternal punishment. *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="20" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 20:10</span>)*

The scriptures describe the work and activity of the devil as:

- Opposes God’s Work *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Thessalonians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Thessalonians 2:18</span>)*

- Deceives the World *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="12" data-verses="9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 12:9</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Corinthians" data-chapter="4" data-verses="4" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Corinthians 4:4</span>)*

- Tempts and Accuses Believers *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Luke" data-chapter="22" data-verses="31" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Luke 22:31</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="12" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 12:10</span>)*

- Afflicts and Harms *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Job" data-chapter="1" data-verses="12" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Job 1:12</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Luke" data-chapter="13" data-verses="16" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Luke 13:16</span>)*

There are many names and descriptions for the devil, each describing a different aspect of his work.

<colgroup>

<col style="width: 31%" />
</colgroup>

</tr>
</thead>

<tr>
<td><strong>Title</strong></td>
<td><strong>Meaning</strong></td>
<td><strong>References</strong></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Satan</strong></td>
<td>"Adversary," opposing God and His people.</td>
<td><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Job" data-chapter="1" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Job 1:6</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Peter" data-chapter="5" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Peter 5:8</span></td>
</tr>

<td><strong>Devil</strong></td>
<td>"Accuser" or "Slanderer."</td>
<td><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="12" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 12:10</span></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Serpent</strong></td>
<td>Deceiver and tempter.</td>

</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Prince of this World</strong></td>
<td>Rules over worldly systems opposed to God.</td>
<td><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="12" data-verses="31" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 12:31</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Corinthians" data-chapter="4" data-verses="4" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Corinthians 4:4</span></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Tempter</strong></td>
<td>Seeks to lead people into sin.</td>
<td><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="4" data-verses="3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 4:3</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Thessalonians" data-chapter="3" data-verses="5" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Thessalonians 3:5</span></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Father of Lies</strong></td>
<td>Misleads through deception.</td>

</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

The gospels and epistles further clarify the work of the enemy. Evil spirits, as disembodied, largely must do their work through willing and open vessels. God works through people, so does the devil. This oppression is often marked by torment, self-harm, and destructive behaviors *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Luke" data-chapter="8" data-verses="27-29" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Luke 8:27-29</span>).* Their attacks cause fear, temptation, and discouragement *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Corinthians" data-chapter="12" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Corinthians 12:7</span>).* The most common source of their power (not authority) in someone's life is through the influence of lies and emotional strongholds *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Corinthians" data-chapter="10" data-verses="3-5" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Corinthians 10:3-5</span>).*

It's important to note that not every lie a person believes, infirmity in their body, or stronghold in their life is the direct result of demonic influence, but these places of woundedness are places where the enemy can exploit us and keep us in a place of frustration and oppression.

#### Jesus’ Authority Over the Works of the Enemy

Do not fear, Jesus has complete and total authority over the works of the enemy. Jesus Demonstrated power over demons during His ministry *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="1" data-verses="34" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Mark 1:34</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="5" data-verses="1-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Mark 5:1-20</span>).* The works of the enemy and Satan himself are defeated through Jesus’ death and resurrection *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Colossians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="15" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Colossians 2:15</span>).*

As believers we have been given authority delegated by Jesus to cast out demons in His name:


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Mark" data-chapter="16" data-verses="17" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Mark 16:17</u>** (NKJV) …In My name they will cast out demons…

</span>


Furthermore, as heirs of salvation we have the authority to submit ourselves to God, resist the devil and He will flee from us! (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="James" data-chapter="4" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">James 4:7</span>).

To deal with an evil or unclean spirit the pattern expressed in the scriptures is to take authority over it in the name of Jesus and command it to leave. The Holy Spirit promises to give us wisdom and discernment if we ask! (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="James" data-chapter="1" data-verses="5" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">James 1:5</span>). We don’t need to make it a production, or a drama like in the movies, or to even talk with them, that is a trick of the devil to distract us. Satan's power is established and maintained only by the lies He can get us to believe. 

<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="18" data-verses="18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Matthew 18:18</u>** (NKJV) whatever we bind on earth will be bound in heaven

</span>


We speak to them in the name of Jesus and command them to leave.

There are two major cautions in the scriptures when dealing with the demonic:

1.  **Only those with a relationship with Jesus have the authority over the demonic**. <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="19" data-verses="11-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Acts 19:11-20</span> talks about sons of a Jewish Chief Priest named Sceva. They tried to intervene in the life of someone who was oppressed and tormented by the devil and the evil spirit refused to leave because they didn’t have a relationship with Jesus. The man who was oppressed leapt on them, stripped them naked and beat them. Just as you should not impersonate a police officer, **don't impersonate someone with a relationship with Jesus, instead have a relationship with Jesus**!

2.  **When an evil spirit leaves, it goes away for a time and will try to come back**. If it finds the place it had dwelled empty, it will come back worse. <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="12" data-verses="43-45" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 12:43-45</span>. When we minister to a person oppressed of the devil, that person needs to fill the void that evil spirit occupied in their lives. That means at a minimum establishing a personal relationship with Jesus and strengthening it by obedience to the Holy Spirit. Often an evil or unclean spirit has power to operate in someone’s life through the choices they made, based on their beliefs. Anyone delivered from the devil needs to replace that old belief system and be transformed by the renewal of their minds (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Romans" data-chapter="12" data-verses="1-2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Romans 12:1-2</span>).

We serve an awesome and mighty God, and we are ‘more than conquerors’ (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Romans" data-chapter="8" data-verses="37" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Romans 8:37</span>) over the defeated enemy. We have no need to fear our defeated enemy, for Jesus has given us His authority to crush his head! The devil’s time is short, and He will ultimately be judged along with every principality and power on the earth that rejects the dominion of Christ.

### Acknowledgements

The section on Angels & Demons was, by permission, based on a prior outline and manuscript by Kingsway Fellowship from their course: “*Foundational Doctrine 1”*.

## **Personal Reflection Questions** 

1.  What does it mean to you that Jesus has all authority in heaven and on earth? How does this truth shape your faith?

2.  Reflect on the concept of delegated authority. How does understanding that your authority comes from Christ influence how you exercise it?

3.  Have you experienced spiritual authority in your life-either by exercising it or witnessing it? How did it impact your faith?

4.  What areas of your life have you struggled to surrender to Christ’s authority? How can submitting these areas lead to freedom and peace?

5.  What specific area of your life or ministry do you sense God calling you to exercise His authority? How can you take a step of faith in this area?

## Group Discussion Questions (20-30 Minutes)

1.  **Icebreaker Question:** What’s an example of someone you’ve seen walking confidently in spiritual authority? What stood out to you about their life or actions?

2.  **Exploring Scripture:** Read <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="18-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 28:18-20</span>. How does Jesus’ authority form the foundation of the Great Commission? What does this mean for us as disciples?

3.  **The Nature of Authority:** What is the difference between power and authority? Why is it important to understand this distinction as believers?

4.  **Angels, Demons, and Spiritual Warfare:** How does understanding the role of angels and demons influence the way we approach spiritual warfare?

5.  **Practical Application:** How can we use spiritual authority to pray for others, resist the enemy, or bring healing and restoration? Share examples or ideas.

# Chapter 14 - Keep Watch

Having been given the spiritual authority of Christ on the Earth to establish His kingdom we should work with diligence and haste, for Jesus will return!

The return of Christ is an important part of our faith. The study of the return of Christ and the end times is known as **eschatology**. Many books on eschatology have been written, and *most of them disagree with each other*! Specifically, they disagree on interpretations (figurative, literal, symbolic), the order and timings of the events in the scriptures. I'm reminded of the saying by the great Pentecostal evangelist Reinhard Bonke - “I will split hairs in heaven when I am done breaking chains on Earth!”

As the author, I’m hesitant to dive very deep into this hotly debated topic given that this book is focused primarily on fundamentals of the faith. I will stick largely to the highest-level view of the events depicted in scripture and focus on what matters most - **the** **mission of the Gospel**!

## Universally Agreed Upon Points:

1.  Jesus Is Returning

2.  Resurrection of the Dead

3.  Judgment for All People

4.  Eternal Destiny

5.  A Defeated Enemy

6.  New Heaven and New Earth

For each point we will review what the concept is, look at reference scriptures, consider why it matters, and what we should do in light of it.

### **1. Jesus Is Returning**

**What It Is:** The **Second Coming of Christ** is the literal return of Jesus to the earth as King and Judge.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Acts" data-chapter="1" data-verses="11" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Acts 1:11</u>** (NKJV) *Men of Galilee, why do you stand gazing up into heaven? This same Jesus, who was taken up from you into heaven, will so come in like manner as you saw Him go into heaven.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="22" data-verses="12" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Revelation 22:12</u>***, (NKJV) And behold, I am coming quickly, and My reward is with Me, to give to every one according to his work.*

</span>


**Why It Matters:** Christ’s return fulfills God’s promise to complete redemption and establish His kingdom. It reminds us to live with urgency, knowing we will one day see Him face-to-face.

**What Should We Do?**

- **Be Watchful:** Live in expectation of His return.

- **Stay Faithful:** Carry out the Great Commission (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="28" data-verses="19-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 28:19-20</span>) with purpose and diligence.

### **2. Resurrection of the Dead**

**What It Is:** The **resurrection** is the raising of all people-both righteous and unrighteous-from the dead to face **judgment**.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="John" data-chapter="5" data-verses="28-29" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>John 5:28-29</u>** *(NKJV) **28** Do not marvel at this; for the hour is coming in which all who are in the graves will hear His voice **29** and come forth-those who have done good, to the resurrection of life, and those who have done evil, to the resurrection of condemnation.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="1 Thessalonians" data-chapter="4" data-verses="16" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>1 Thessalonians 4:16</u>** *(NKJV) *… the dead in Christ will rise first.*

</span>


**Why It Matters:** The resurrection guarantees justice and eternal life for believers while warning unbelievers of judgment. It points to God’s sovereignty over life and death.

**What Should We Do?**

- **Be Prepared:** Live a life of repentance and faithfulness to God.

- **Share the Gospel:** Help others prepare for the resurrection of life by leading them to Christ.

### **3. Judgment for All People**

**What It Is:** All people-believers and unbelievers-will face judgment before God. Believers appear at the **Judgment Seat of Christ** for rewards, while unbelievers face the **Great White Throne Judgment**.

#### Great White Throne Judgement


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="20" data-verses="11-12" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Revelation 20:11-12</u>** *(NKJV) **11** Then I saw a great white throne … **12** … And the dead were judged according to their works*

</span>


As described in the chapter on sin, there will be a great and dreadful final judgement for all non-believers. The punishment will be eternal separation from God! **This judgement is not for the believer.**

#### Judgement Seat of Christ

As believers our names have been put in the **Lamb's *Book of Life*** *(*See the book of *Revelation)**.** As such we will stand* *the **Judgement Seat of Christ***. This judgement is not for condemnation, but rather for reward! Again**,** it is not a moment of **shame or fear** for believers, but a **celebration** of what God has accomplished through us. It reminds us that our lives matter-not just for today but for **eternity**.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="2 Corinthians" data-chapter="5" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>2 Corinthians 5:10</u>** (NKJV) *For we must all appear before the judgment seat of Christ, that each one may receive the things done in the body, according to what he has done, whether good or bad.*

</span>



<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="1 Corinthians" data-chapter="3" data-verses="12-15" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>1 Corinthians 3:12-15</u>** (NKJV)
>
> ***12** Now if anyone builds on this foundation with gold, silver, precious stones, wood, hay, straw,*
>
> ***13** each one's work will become clear; for the Day will declare it, because it will be revealed by fire; and the fire will test each one's work, of what sort it is.*
>
> ***14** If anyone's work which he has built on it endures, he will receive a reward.*
>
> ***15** If anyone's work is burned, he will suffer loss; but he himself will be saved, yet so as through fire.*

</span>


At this judgement we will receive rewards for the good works we have done! Everyone at this judgement lived and died as a believer, so their eternal salvation is guaranteed. However not everyone will have further reward. The New Testament mentions crowns as rewards that represent victory, honor, and faithfulness. These laurel crowns highlight God’s recognition of believers’ faithfulness and service in His Kingdom.

The rewards:

1.  **The Crown of Righteousness** *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Timothy" data-chapter="4" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Timothy 4:8</span>)* - For those who long for Christ’s appearing and live godly lives.

2.  **The Incorruptible Crown** *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Corinthians" data-chapter="9" data-verses="24-25" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Corinthians 9:24-25</span>)* - For believers who practice self-discipline and faithfulness.

3.  **The Crown of Life** *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="James" data-chapter="1" data-verses="12" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">James 1:12</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="2" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 2:10</span>)* - For those who endure trials, persecution, and testing.

4.  **The Crown of Glory** *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Peter" data-chapter="5" data-verses="2-4" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Peter 5:2-4</span>)* - For leaders and shepherds who faithfully guide God’s people.

5.  **The Crown of Rejoicing** *(<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Thessalonians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="19-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Thessalonians 2:19-20</span>)* - For those who lead others to Christ and make disciples.

**What Should We Do?**

- **Live with Purpose:** Work diligently, knowing our efforts for the Kingdom matter eternally.

- **Examine Ourselves:** Ensure we live lives of faith and obedience.

### **4. Eternal Destiny**

**What It Is:** All people will spend eternity in one of two places-eternal life with God or eternal separation from Him.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="25" data-verses="46" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Matthew 25:46</u>** (NKJV) And these will go away into everlasting punishment, but the righteous into eternal life.*

</span>


**Why It Matters:** Our eternal destiny is determined by whether we have accepted or rejected Christ. It emphasizes the urgency of the Gospel message and discipleship.

**What Should We Do?**

- **Proclaim Salvation:** Share the Gospel boldly to lead others to eternal life.

- **Live with Eternity in Mind:** Focus on what matters eternally-faith, obedience, and love.

### **5. A Defeated Enemy**

**What It Is:** Satan, the deceiver, will be cast into **the lake of fire**-defeated forever by Christ.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="20" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Revelation 20:10</u>**, (NKJV) *The devil, who deceived them, was cast into the lake of fire and brimstone where the beast and the false prophet are. And they will be tormented day and night forever and ever.*

</span>


**Why It Matters:** This final act demonstrates God’s sovereignty over evil and assures believers that Satan’s reign of deception and destruction is over. It gives us confidence in God’s ultimate victory.

**What Should We Do?**

- **Stand Firm:** Resist the enemy’s lies, knowing He is already defeated.

- **Walk in Authority:** Live with boldness and faith, knowing God has already won the victory.

### **6. New Heaven and New Earth**

**What It Is:** After the final judgment and defeat of the enemy, God will create a **new heaven and new earth**-a perfect eternal dwelling place for His people.


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="fragment" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="21" data-verses="1,3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Revelation 21:1,3</u>** *(NKJV) **1** Now I saw a new heaven and a new earth… **3** …God Himself will be with them and be their God.*

</span>


**Why It Matters:** It gives us hope for restoration, where God will wipe away every tear and make all things new-free from pain, sin, and death.

**What Should We Do?**

- **Fix Our Eyes on Eternity:** Keep our focus on the eternal hope rather than temporary struggles.

- **Persevere in Faith:** Endure trials, knowing that eternal joy awaits.

## Points of Common Disagreement

For the sake of completeness, should you be curious about the common division points are in eschatology, here is a **very** brief overview of the most frequently debated points.

- **Rapture Timing:** Pre-, Mid-, or Post-Tribulation? Views differ on **when** the rapture occurs relative to the **tribulation**. Some denominations don’t believe in the rapture.

- **Tribulation Purpose:** Is it literal judgment for Israel or a symbolic period of struggle?

- **Millennial Reign:** Is it literal **(Premillennial)**, symbolic **(Amillennial)**, or spiritual **(Postmillennial)**?

- **Satan’s Final Rebellion:** Happens after the millennium (literal event or figurative?).

It is great to study eschatology, but let's remember as D.L. Browning said, “Always keep the main thing, the main thing!”

## **Final Thoughts**

The return of Christ is the culmination of God’s plan and the reason we live and labor with urgency and purpose. The parables of the ten virgins (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="25" data-verses="1-13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 25:1-13</span>) and the two men in the field (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="24" data-verses="40-42" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 24:40-42</span>) remind us to be watchful and faithful, always prepared for His return. Whether through the rewards of crowns or the hope of resurrection, our motivation is clear-Christ is coming back!

**May we be found ready, faithful, and expectant on that glorious day!**

## Personal Reflection Questions 

1.  How does the promise of Christ’s return influence the way you live your daily life? Are there areas where you need to shift your focus or priorities?

2.  Reflect on the parable of the ten virgins (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="25" data-verses="1-13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 25:1-13</span>). How can you ensure your “lamp” is prepared and full? What does that look like practically?

3.  What does it mean to you to live as a faithful servant, ready for Christ’s return? How can you grow in faithfulness?

4.  In what specific ways can you live today with an eternal perspective, focusing on what truly matters considering Christ’s return?

## Group Discussion Questions (20-30 Minutes)

1.  **Icebreaker Question:** “If you knew Jesus was returning tomorrow, what’s the first thing you’d do today?”

2.  **Exploring Scripture:** Read <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="24" data-verses="42-44" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 24:42-44</span>. What does it mean to ‘keep watch’? How can we balance readiness with the demands of everyday life?

3.  **Faithful Living:** Discuss the parable of the two men in the field (<span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="24" data-verses="40-41" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 24:40-41</span>). How does this illustrate the suddenness of Christ’s return? How can we live prepared for that moment?

4.  **Crowns and Rewards:** What do the crowns mentioned in Scripture (e.g., Crown of Righteousness, Crown of Life) teach us about God’s recognition of our faithfulness? How should this shape our lives?

5.  **Encouraging One Another:** How can we, as a group or community, encourage one another to stay focused on Christ and live in anticipation of His return?

# Postscript

As I bring this book to a close, my hope and prayer for you, dear reader, is that these foundational truths have strengthened your faith, deepened your understanding, and drawn you closer to God. Doctrine is not merely an intellectual pursuit-it is the framework upon which we build our lives, shaping how we live, love, and serve in the Kingdom of God.

If there is one thing I would leave you with, it is this: Keep growing. Keep seeking. Keep walking in faith. The truths outlined in this book are not meant to be read once and set aside; they are meant to be lived out daily. The journey of following Christ is one of continual learning, refining, and transformation. We move from faith to faith, from glory to glory, ever pressing on to know Him more.

You may have questions that remain unanswered. You may feel like you’ve only scratched the surface of these deep and eternal truths. That is a good place to be. Let those questions drive you deeper into the Word of God, into prayer, and into fellowship with other believers. The Holy Spirit is your guide, your teacher, and your comforter-He will lead you into all truth as you seek Him.

Paul wrote


<span class="x-scripture" data-type="quote" data-book="Philippians" data-chapter="3" data-verses="12-14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">

> **<u>Philippians 3:12-14</u>** (NASB):
>
>*"Not that I have already grasped it all or have already become perfect, but I press on if I may also take hold of that for which I was even taken hold of by Christ Jesus. Brothers and sisters, I do not regard myself as having taken hold of it yet; but one thing I do: forgetting what lies behind and reaching forward to what lies ahead, I press on toward the goal for the prize of the upward call of God in Christ Jesus."*

</span>


Let us press on together. Stand firm in sound doctrine, walk boldly in faith, and proclaim the Gospel with confidence. The world desperately needs the light of Christ, and you, equipped with the truth, are called to shine.

Thank you for allowing me to be a part of your journey. My prayer is that you will continue to grow in grace, stand in truth, and be a faithful disciple of Jesus Christ.

With faith and expectation,

**Andrew D. Nieuwsma**

<td><p><strong>I AM THAT I AM</strong></p>
<p>Eh-YEH a-sher Eh-YEH</p></td>

<p>Eh-loh-HEEM</p></td>

<td>Emphasizes God’s power and majesty as Creator; a plural form denoting greatness or the Trinity.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh (Jehovah)</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh (jeh-HO-vah)</p></td>
<td><p>LORD, "I AM"</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="3" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 3:14</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="42" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Isaiah 42:8</span></p></td>
<td>The covenant name of God, indicating His eternal, self-existent nature, and faithfulness to Israel.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Adonai</strong></p>
<p>Ah-doh-NIGH</p></td>
<td><p>Lord, Master</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="15" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 15:2</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="16" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 16:2</span></p></td>
<td>Reflects God's authority and lordship, acknowledging Him as the rightful ruler and owner of creation.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>El Shaddai</strong></p>
<p>El shad-DYE</p></td>
<td><p>God Almighty, All-Sufficient One</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="17" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 17:1</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="91" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 91:1</span></p></td>
<td>Signifies God’s power to nourish, bless, and provide abundantly; shows His sufficiency.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Jireh</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh YI-rah</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD Will Provide</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="22" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 22:14</span></p></td>
<td>Symbolizes God’s provision; first used when God provided a ram as a substitute for Isaac.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Rapha</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh RAH-fah</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD Who Heals</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="15" data-verses="26" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 15:26</span></p></td>
<td>Shows God’s healing nature; He restores physical, emotional, and spiritual health.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Nissi</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh NEE-see</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD is My Banner</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="17" data-verses="15" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 17:15</span></p></td>
<td>Represents God’s protection and victory; used by Moses after a victory over the Amalekites.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-M’kaddesh</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh Meh-KAH-desh</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD Who Sanctifies</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Leviticus" data-chapter="20" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Leviticus 20:8</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="31" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 31:13</span></p></td>
<td>Points to God as the One who sets His people apart as holy, through His commandments and grace.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Shalom</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh shah-LOME</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD is Peace</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Judges" data-chapter="6" data-verses="24" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Judges 6:24</span></p></td>
<td>Gideon used this name after God’s reassurance; highlights God as the source of peace and wholeness.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Sabaoth</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh Sa-ba-OTE</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD of Hosts (Armies)</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Samuel" data-chapter="1" data-verses="3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Samuel 1:3</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="46" data-verses="7" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 46:7</span></p></td>
<td>Refers to God’s supreme power over all heavenly and earthly forces; emphasizes His sovereignty.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Raah</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh RAH-ah</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD is My Shepherd</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="23" data-verses="1" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 23:1</span></p></td>
<td>David’s use of this name to show God’s guidance, care, and provision, like a shepherd with His flock.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Tsidkenu</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh Tsid-KAY-noo</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD Our Righteousness</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Jeremiah" data-chapter="23" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Jeremiah 23:6</span></p></td>

</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Shammah</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh SHAH-mah</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD is There</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Ezekiel" data-chapter="48" data-verses="35" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Ezekiel 48:35</span></p></td>

<tr>
<td><p><strong>El Elyon</strong></p>
<p>El EL-yawn</p></td>
<td><p>God Most High</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="14" data-verses="18-20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 14:18-20</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="57" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 57:2</span></p></td>

<tr>
<td><p><strong>El Olam</strong></p>
<p>El OH-lahm</p></td>
<td><p>Everlasting God</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="21" data-verses="33" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 21:33</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="40" data-verses="28" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Isaiah 40:28</span></p></td>
<td>Emphasizes God’s eternal nature; He exists beyond time and is unchanging.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p><strong>Yahweh-Makkeh</strong></p>
<p>YAH-weh MAH-keh</p></td>
<td><p>The LORD Who Strikes</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Ezekiel" data-chapter="7" data-verses="9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Ezekiel 7:9</span>, 22:31</p></td>

<tr>
<td><strong>Yahweh-Gmolah<br />
</strong>YAH-weh GIM-oh-lah</td>
<td><p>The God of Recompense</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Jeremiah" data-chapter="51" data-verses="56" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Jeremiah 51:56</span></p></td>

<p>KAH-nah</p></td>
<td><p>Jealous</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="34" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 34:14</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Deuteronomy" data-chapter="4" data-verses="24" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Deuteronomy 4:24</span></p></td>

<tr>
<td><p><strong>El Roi</strong></p>
<p>El ROY-ee</p></td>

<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Genesis" data-chapter="16" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Genesis 16:13</span></p></td>

<p>YAH-weh khan-NOON</p></td>

<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="86" data-verses="15" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 86:15</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="34" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 34:6</span></p></td>

<p>YAH-weh EL-yawn</p></td>

</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Faithful</strong></p>
<p>Relational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Corinthians" data-chapter="10" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Corinthians 10:13</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="2 Timothy" data-chapter="2" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">2 Timothy 2:13</span></p></th>
<th>Completely trustworthy, with promises that will never fail.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Merciful</strong></p>
<p>Relational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Titus" data-chapter="3" data-verses="5" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Titus 3:5</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="103" data-verses="8-18" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 103:8-18</span></p></th>
<th>Divine goodness expressed in relieving His creatures’ suffering and showing patience with sinners.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Compassion</strong></p>
<p>Relational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Lamentations" data-chapter="3" data-verses="22-23" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Lamentations 3:22-23</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="145" data-verses="8-9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 145:8-9</span></p></th>
<th>God feels deeply for human suffering, responding with empathy and care.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Good</strong></p>
<p>Moral</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="25" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 25:8</span></p></th>
<th>Completely good, though evil exists due to free will and natural law.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Just</strong></p>
<p>Moral</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="89" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 89:14</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Deuteronomy" data-chapter="32" data-verses="4" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Deuteronomy 32:4</span></p></th>
<th>God ensures justice, addressing wrongs and rewarding righteousness.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Jealous</strong></p>
<p>Moral</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Exodus" data-chapter="34" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Exodus 34:14</span></p></th>
<th>Protective love that desires exclusive devotion from His people.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Righteous</strong></p>
<p>Moral</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="11" data-verses="3-4" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Isaiah 11:3-4</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 John" data-chapter="1" data-verses="9" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 John 1:9</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Amos" data-chapter="3" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Amos 3:2</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="61" data-verses="10" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Isaiah 61:10</span></p></th>
<th>Conforms to a perfect standard, condemning the wicked and pardoning the penitent.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Holy</strong></p>
<p>Moral</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Isaiah" data-chapter="6" data-verses="3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Isaiah 6:3</span></p></th>
<th>Perfectly pure, set apart from sin, unable to tolerate it.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Sovereign</strong></p>
<p>Foundational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Daniel" data-chapter="4" data-verses="35" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Daniel 4:35</span></p></th>
<th>Has the authority to govern and dispose of His creation as He pleases.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Wise</strong></p>
<p>Foundational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Colossians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="2-3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Colossians 2:2-3</span></p></th>
<th>Always acts rightly, at the right time, in the right way.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Omniscient</strong></p>
<p>Foundational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="147" data-verses="5" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 147:5</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="6" data-verses="3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 6:3</span></p></th>
<th>Perfect in knowledge, understanding all things instantaneously without needing to learn.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Omnipresent</strong></p>
<p>Foundational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Proverbs" data-chapter="15" data-verses="3" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Proverbs 15:3</span></p></th>
<th>Present everywhere, observing all things, but He dwells personally where He is in relationship.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Omnipotent</strong> Foundational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Job" data-chapter="42" data-verses="2" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Job 42:2</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="19" data-verses="26" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 19:26</span></p></th>
<th>All-powerful, with freedom to do all consistent with His nature; cannot act contrary to His nature.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Immutable</strong> Foundational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Psalm" data-chapter="102" data-verses="27" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Psalm 102:27</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Hebrews" data-chapter="13" data-verses="8" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Hebrews 13:8</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="James" data-chapter="1" data-verses="17" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">James 1:17</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Malachi" data-chapter="3" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Malachi 3:6</span></p></th>
<th>Unchanging in nature and character, remaining the same eternally.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>Infinite</strong></p>
<p>Foundational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="1 Kings" data-chapter="8" data-verses="27" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">1 Kings 8:27</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Deuteronomy" data-chapter="33" data-verses="27" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Deuteronomy 33:27</span></p></th>
<th>Not limited by space or time, existing everywhere and eternally.</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th><p><strong>God is a Spirit</strong> Foundational</p>
<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="4" data-verses="24" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 4:24</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="5" data-verses="37" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 5:37</span>; <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Philippians" data-chapter="2" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Philippians 2:6</span></p></th>
<th>God is a Spirit with personality, having thoughts, feelings, and communion with His creatures. He has no material body but is a real, infinite being.</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
</tbody>
</table>

The attributes are grouped into relational, moral, foundational, ability categories. Relational attributes describe how we personally experience God. Moral attributes describe His character. Foundational attributes describe His essential nature of being.

<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="1" data-verses="29" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 1:29</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Revelation" data-chapter="5" data-verses="6" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Revelation 5:6</span></p></td>

<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Matthew" data-chapter="8" data-verses="20" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Matthew 8:20</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Daniel" data-chapter="7" data-verses="13-14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Daniel 7:13-14</span></p></td>

<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Galatians" data-chapter="3" data-verses="13" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Galatians 3:13</span>, <span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="Titus" data-chapter="2" data-verses="14" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">Titus 2:14</span></p></td>

<p><span class="x-scripture" data-type="ref" data-book="John" data-chapter="6" data-verses="35" data-lang="en" data-version="NKJV">John 6:35</span></p></td>
